10 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. -[APRIL-JUNE, (Recived ist August 1904.) CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWOHWANG, 13TH JULY 1904. SIR, I.-The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil A istration having opened, on the 12th August 19oo, a Custom House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistics o that trade from rat April to 3oth June 1904. On all cargoes arriving or leavihg by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were colleated at Treaty Tariff rates, and Likin at the rate of 19 per cent ad vadorem on Imports and 1f per cent. ad valorem on Exports, with the exception of the Duties on certain specified staples, which were reduced on Tst May 19or to the rate formerly assessed by the Native Cnstoms. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. TR following information, received officially, regarding action taken or contemplated by the Russian Military and Naval Authorities in the North, is hereby notified for general information and guidance:- 1. Submarine mines having been laid at the mouth of the Liao River, -vessels entering the Port of Newehwang are required to observe the regulations the Russian Authorities have enacted. 2 If vessels do not show lights within the fighting sphere by night or flags by day, or fail to reply when warned by a gun to answer questions, they will be treated as enemies, and sunk. SHANoHAI, i6th Apri i904.

250 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, . No. 896. RUSS0-JAPANESE .WAR. WARNING TO VESSELS BY RUSSIAN AUTHORITIES. THE following Notification, issued by the Harbour Master at Newchwang, is hereby published for general information:- HAROUR NoTIFCATIoN No. 8. &quot;In continuation of Haibour Notifications Nos. 2 and 6, notice is hereby given, at the request of the Civil Administrator, that:- i Incoming steamers arriving off the Bar after dusk must anchor outside the Bar and in no ease cross the Bar before daylight. At daylight pilots will proceed to meet the steamers and bring them in for examination by Military Authorities off the East Spit and subsequently to their berths. &quot;2. Outgoing steamers&#39; must leave the port in broad daylight in order to have time to cross the Bar without stopping in the river. Steamers which for some reason are unable to cross the Bar must return to the harbour and are not under any circumstances to remain in the river, i.e., below the Fort, during&#39;the night. &quot;3&#39;. Pilots must not under any circumstances remain with their boats or steam- launh near the Bar, i.e., below the Fort, during the night. They must return to the harbour before dusk. &quot;J. In conformity with the instructions contained.in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at. Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessel*for the second quarter of the year 1904. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan ( g), Lintin (f) and Shamchun (g JJ), on the north and west; Samun (2 I9), on the Ast; and Shaiichung (8 ) and Shatowkok (8 J ), on the Mirs Bay littoral. 296 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Beceived 26th July 1904.) CUSTOM HOUSE) KIAOCHOW, 19TH JULY 1904. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of thiB port for the second quarter of the year 1904. b. The Custom House (, 3) was opened on the Ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import anel Export Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Th 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and ikin on JiAnk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled by the Kiaochow Customs are known as Tsangkou (&#39; 0); Nuku ( ); Shatsazkou ( f ), including Tengyao (H u); Hungshihyai (S&#39; W g); Taputou (&amp;amp; * ); and Lingshanwei (3 (1) ), including TachiangkonuA ) c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, the statistics of goods landed and&#39;consumed in German territory not being included.
